## A343 The prognostic value of proliferation index in pulmonary carcinoid tumours

## Amber Ahmed-Issap, Alina Budacan, Rana Mehdi, Kajan Mahendran, Lakshmi Srinivasan, Shilajit Ghosh, Udo Abah

## University Hospital of North Midlands, Stoke-on-Trent, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: Alina Budacan
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A343
Background: Carcinoid tumours represent approximately 2% of all lung tumours. In pulmonary surgical oncology prognosis is typically discussed in terms of typical versus atypical. Little importance is placed on proliferative index, which is commonly used to predict prognosis in other primary carcinoid sites. We reviewed of our institutional data to exam the relationship of proliferative index and recurrence following resection of pulmonary carcinoid tumours.
Methods: Data was extracted from a prospectively filled surgical database to identify all patients who underwent resection for pulmonary carcinoids between 2012 and 2021. Patient’s demographics, perioperative data and histological data were examined.
Results: 116 patients were identified, 69% of patients were female and the average age was 65.6 years. 106 patients had proliferative index reported on; of these 16 patients had atypical carcinoid and 90 typical carcinoid tumours resected. Recurrence rates stratified by histological diagnosis were 44% in the atypical group and 6% in the typical group (P < 0.05). Recurrence rates stratified by proliferation index ranges 0–2%, > 2%-10%, and > 10% were 6.3%, 8.3%, and 71.4%, respectively (P < 0.05). Within the atypical group 75% of patients had a proliferation index of > 10% and in the typical cohort all patients bar one had a proliferation index < 5%. The average proliferation index in the atypical carcinoid group was 12.7% and 2.39% in typical carcinoid group.
Conclusion: The use of proliferative index adds additional prognostic information to the histological classification of carcinoid tumours. A proliferation rate > 10% has a significant risk of recurrence warranting an increased frequency of postoperative surveillance.

## A344 Thoracic Endovascular Aortic Repair (TEVAR) for chronic type B aortic dissection. A systematic review of contemporary outcomes

## Katie O'Sullivan, Jonathan Strickland, Gemma McKevitt, Stephen Badger, Anton Collins, Reuben Jeganathan, Alsir Ahmed

## Royal Victoria Hospital, Belfast, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: Jonathan Strickland
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A344
Objective: Thoracic endovascular aortic repair (TEVAR) is a rapidly expanding field and has been transformational in the management of a range of aortic pathologies in recent years. TEVAR is now considered a standard of care for complicated acute type B aortic dissection however, the management of chronic type B dissection is a more complex issue. While traditionally these patients were managed with best medical therapy the use of TEVAR is growing. In view of this evolving and important application of TEVAR, the purpose of this systematic review is to provide a contemporary update of the procedural outcomes.
Methods: A systematic review of the current literature to identify outcomes of TEVAR for chronic type B aortic dissection was undertaken. To ensure a review of contemporary practice, studies published 2016 -2021 were included.
Results: A total of 1,248 patients were identified from 17 studies. Indications for TEVAR varied but typically encompassed aneurysmal expansion > 55 mm, rapid expansion, malperfusion, pain, rupture, or refractory hypertension. Overall rate of left subclavian artery (LSA) coverage was 31.6%, access complications 3.3%, acute kidney injury 3.1%, malperfusion 2.7%. Incidence of stroke was 1.2% and paraplegia 1.8%. The overall 30-day mortality rate was 3%. Overall rates of reintervention and retrograde dissection were 10.9 and 2% respectively. Overall rate of endoleak was 10.9%.
Conclusions: Contemporary outcomes of TEVAR for chronic type B dissection in studies examined to date are acceptable. Ongoing examination of this subgroup of patients is required with focus on categories of chronicity.

## A345 Virtual online consultations: should we still be assessing new thoracic surgical patients via video consultation?

## Charlotte Hope, Sofina Begum

## Royal Brompton Hospital, London, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: Charlotte Hope
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A345
Introduction: Video consultations have become routine since the COVID-19 pandemic. New patient consultations are of high value to the surgical team, as they allow a full history and physical assessment, which is of high importance during the preoperative assessment of the patients. Video consultation can be advantageous for both patients and healthcare providers. However, it can also be seen as clinically risky and less acceptable to patients. Literature on video consultations remains limited, therefore in a post-pandemic era should we continue to see new patients by video consultation?
Aims: To evaluate clinicians' and new patients' experience of initial consultation via video compared to in-person.
Method: Surveys were developed and piloted for clinicians and new patients. All new patients in the thoracic surgery outpatient clinic who are scheduled for video consultation between October 30th 2023 and December 11th 2023 will be invited to complete a patient satisfaction questionnaire at the end of their consultation.
Results: We hypothesise patients who are technically capable and with significant travel distance will prefer video consultations. We anticipate overall patient choice will be a favourable outcome of the survey. Results are due end of December 2023.
Discussion: Since the COVID-19 pandemic, video consultation has provided a valuable consultation modality for patients and healthcare professionals. However, there are also clinical limitations. Therefore, we review whether we should continue to see new patients via video consultation. We anticipate the overall outcome will favour patient choice in their assessment modality.

## A345 Surgical explantation of transcatheter aortic valves: a single-centre experience and systematic review of the literature

## Riccardo Giuseppe Abbasciano1, Panagiotis Theodoropoulos1, Carla Lucarelli2, Thanos Athanasiou1, Alessandro Viviano1

## 1Imperial College Healthcare NHS Trust, London, United Kingdom. 2HCA Healthcare, London, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: Carla Lucarelli
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A345
Background: Indication to surgical explantation of TAVR is becoming increasingly more frequent, due to the higher number of procedures performed in patients with longer life expectancy. We performed a systematic review and meta-analysis with meta-regression to identify factors that can determine an increase in the high mortality and morbidity that characterize these surgical procedures. We reviewed the outcomes from our experience to compare them with those published in literature.
Methods: MEDLINE and Embase were searched for relevant studies. Twelve studies were eligible according to our inclusion criteria. Standard Cochrane methods were adopted.
We reviewed the outcomes from our series of patients operated in the period between 2022 and 2023.
Results: TAVR explantation was confirmed as a procedure with high mortality 30-days (0.17; 95% CI, 0.14–0.21) and morbidity (stroke incidence 5%; 95% CI, 0.04–0.07; kidney injury incidence 16%; 95% CI, 0.11–0.24). The type of transcatheter valve implanted during the index procedure, the requirement for aortic reconstruction and the indication for the operation did not influence the outcomes after surgical explantation in the metaregression analysis performed.
Four patients were operated in our centre in the period analysed. We did not record in-hospital mortality or major adverse cardiovascular events. One patient required a pacemaker implantation.
Conclusions: The need for these high-risk operations is growing and it will likely expand in the next years. Specific tools for risk stratification are required. Our internal series showed good short term outcomes, possibly as a result of an effective collaboration with the cardiology service in selecting the patients.

## A346 The effect of peri-operative carotid artery intervention on in-hospital and 30-day outcomes when performed at the time of coronary artery bypass grafting: a systematic review and meta-analysis

## John David Kehoe1,2, Tara Ni Dhonnchu1

## 1Cork University Hospital, Cork, Ireland. 2University College Cork, Cork, Ireland

## Correspondence: John David Kehoe
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A346
Introduction: Cerebrovascular accidents (CVA) remain a devastating complication following coronary artery bypass grafting (CABG). Carotid artery stenosis is a recognised risk factor and may be managed peri-operatively with carotid endarterectomy or stenting. However, previous reviewers have reserved full endorsement of carotid intervention (CI) given the lack of parallel data comparing combined CABG-CI to isolated CABG.
Objectives: The aim of this systematic review and meta-analysis is to evaluate the current literature comparing outcomes of CI to non-interventional controls in those undergoing CABG with severe carotid stenosis.
Methods: A search of two electronic databases (PubMed and EMBASE) was performed in April 2023 for relevant papers published following the year 2000, yielding 1553 potentially relevant studies. All titles were reviewed and eligibility criteria were applied to selected article abstracts, followed by full texts.
Results: Eight studies were eligible for analysis including one randomised clinical trial and seven observational studies, yielding 9,596 patients that underwent combined CABG-CI and 31,989 that underwent CABG alone. CI was found to significantly increase the risk of 30-day CVA (OR 2.5 [1.07,5.85], p = 0.03), in-hospital mortality (OR 1.23 [1.04,1.45], p = 0.01) and 30-day mortality (OR 1.15 [1.02,1.3], p = 0.02), whilst having no effect on the risk of in-hospital CVA (OR 1.2 [0.73,1.95], p = 0.47) or in-hospital TIA (OR 0.92 [0.52,1.62], p = 0.77).
Conclusion: We provide the first review examining the effectiveness of CI compared to non-interventional controls in CABG patients with severe carotid stenosis. Our findings suggest that CI may increase the risk of 30-day adverse outcomes, challenging the validity of this therapeutic approach.

## A347 Outcomes following Post Cardiotomy Veno-Arterial Extra-Corporeal Membrane Oxygenation (PC VA-ECMO) following adult cardiac surgery: a 15-year single-centre experience

## Ahmed Mohamed Abdel Shafi, Jason Ali, Narain Moorjani, David Jenkins, Alain Vuylsteke, Marius Berman, Hassiba Smail

## Royal Papworth Hospital, Cambridge, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: Ahmed Mohamed Abdel Shafi
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A347
Objectives: Cardiac surgery has seen a shift in the patient population with increasing disease severity. Some patients are unable to wean from cardiopulmonary bypass safely following surgery. The increased availability & reliability of ECMO has allowed mechanical circulatory support (MCS) to become a viable option for selected patients in this situation. Our objectives were to assess the outcomes of PC VA-ECMO at our institution.
Methods: This was a retrospective study of all patients that underwent cardiac surgery from January 2008 until July 2023 at our institution from prospectively collected databases. Patients initiated on ECMO prior to surgery, placed on VV-ECMO or VAD were excluded. Patients initiated onto VA-ECMO post cardiotomy were identified and their records analysed.
Results: 28,310 general adult cardiac operations were performed, of which 172 (0.61%) patients fulfilled inclusion criteria with a median age of 66.5 years. Pre-operatively, 59/172 (34.3%) patients had a LVEF < 30%, 42/172 (24.4%) were in cardiogenic shock & 36/172 (20.9%) were on inotropes.
The urgency of the index procedure was elective in 29.1% (50/172), urgent in 29.1% (50/1972) & emergency/salvage in 41.8% (72/172), with 28/172 (16.3%) undergoing a redo-sternotomy. VA-ECMO was instituted at the index operation in 107/172 (62.2%), with the median number of days on ECMO 4.17 days. 9/172 (5.2%) patients went onto further MCS (1-LVAD, 2-BiVAD & 6-RVAD), Table 1 shows complications, mortality & survival outcomes.
Conclusion: Post cardiotomy ECMO is utilised in a small percentage of patients. Although associated with a high mortality & complication rate, its use confers a survival benefit in carefully selected patients to avoid futility. 

## A348 Validation of the STUMBL score for isolated chest trauma at a Major Trauma Centre

## Amber Ahmed-Issap, Daniel Magee, Isabel Soans, Callum Chaney, Nicholas Wong, Kajan Mahendran, Shilajit Ghosh, Lakshmi Srinivasan, Udo Abah

## Royal Stoke Hospital, Stoke-on-Trent, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: Amber Ahmed-Issap
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A348
Objectives: The STUMBL score is a prognostic tool that has been devised for Emergency Medicine (EM) clinicians to predict complications following blunt chest trauma and determine hospital admission. Scores are assigned to five variables: age, number of fractured ribs, chronic lung disease, anticoagulation and oxygen saturation. This study was designed to test the effectiveness of the scoring tool.
Methods: Patients between 01/06/2021 and 12/09/2023 who attended the Emergency Department (ED) of our tertiary hospital were examined. Exclusion criteria included under age 18, ‘old fractures’, patients with missing STUMBL variables, and polytrauma. Patients were divided into three cohorts depending on the STUMBL score: ‘Discharge’ (0–11), ‘Admission’ (12–26) and ‘ICU input’ (≥ 27). Outcomes measured include: infection, haemothorax, pleural effusion, intensive care admission, re-admission and 90-day mortality.
Results: 1867 patients were examined in the ED with blunt chest trauma. 438 patients met the inclusion criteria. Decision to admit by the EM clinician had a sensitivity of 69.9% and specificity of 66.8% in predicting complications. A score of ≥ 12 had a sensitivity of 84.2%, specificity of 31.2% and positive predictive value of 37.7%. The STUMBL score was statistically significantly different in patients who developed complications compared to those without complications (20.5 [14,26] vs 15 [10,20], p < 0.000001), but would not be clinically relevant for admission decision. 146 patients in total developed complications (Table 1). 
Conclusion: The STUMBL score of ≥ 12 to determine admission in preparation for expected complications is higher in sensitivity but much lower in specificity when compared to the ED clinician’s judgement.
Study of the Management of Blunt chest wall trauma (STUMBL) score has been developed to predict complications after rib fractures. It has been advised that patients with a STUMBL score of 0–11 should be discharged, 12-26 should be admitted for monitoring and ≥ 27 should have Intensive Care Unit (ICU) input.

## A349 Is thoracic surgery safe during pregnancy? A single-centre retrospective review of surgical and anaesthetic considerations

## Denis Ajdarpasic, Karishma Chandarana, Mohammad Hawari

## Nottingham University Hospitals NHS Trust, Nottingham, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: Denis Ajdarpasic
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A349
Background: Around 1% of pregnant women undergo non-obstetric surgery in the UK and USA, with the majority of thoracic surgery in a non-elective setting. Maternal outcomes are comparable with non-pregnant patients undergoing similar abdominal and pelvic procedures; however, the literature is limited in addressing outcomes and recommendations are non-specific for pregnant patients undergoing thoracic surgery.
Objectives: We shall discuss surgical and anaesthetic considerations when performing thoracic surgery in pregnant females, focusing on the pre, intra- and postoperative period, and provide recommendations for the thoracic multidisciplinary team in these unfamiliar circumstances.
Methods: A departmental database was used to identify pregnant females who underwent thoracic surgery at our unit over a 25-year period. Data was collected from electronic hospital records and physical notes, identifying measures taken to minimise risk to mother and baby.
Results: Six patients were included in our study with a median age of 35; five patients (83%) were in their third trimester when undergoing thoracic surgery. All patients had surgery in a non-elective setting. All patients received general anaesthesia. Obstetricians were informed in all cases, and physically present during two thoracic procedures (33%).
Special considerations included patient positioning, analgesia, CTG monitoring, administration of steroids and VTE prophylaxis. No maternal or foetal mortality occurred during the inpatient stay.
Conclusions: Thoracic surgery is safe in pregnancy, where multidisciplinary consensus recommends surgical intervention. Individualised management plans with early anaesthetist and obstetrician involvement help to optimise maternal physiology and manage foetal risks; whilst tackling logistical issues such as timing, location and surgical approach. 

## A350 Transforming thoracic trauma care: the impact of a new Major Trauma Centre

## Stamatina Koskolou, Jennifer Williams, Vasileios Valtzoglou, Ainis Pirtnieks, Malgorzata Kornaszewska, Tom Combellack

## University Hospital of Wales, Cardiff, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: Stamatina Koskolou
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A350
Background: Cardiff’s Major Trauma Centre (MTC) started operating in September 2020. This led to improved infrastructure and resources to treat trauma patients, including a specialised multi-disciplinary trauma team and dedicated MTC operating theatres.
Objective: To explore MTC’s impact on thoracic surgery trauma cases.
Methods: We analyzed pain team data from 2016 to 2022 and identified thoracic trauma patients through electronic databases (Theatreman, PATS, WCP) from 2017 to October 2023.
Results: Over the two-year period preceding the MTC launch, pain team referrals for blunt chest trauma increased by 25%, while, over the two-year period following the launch, referrals surged by 127%. Simultaneously, an increase in referrals for thoracic surgical review was noted, with approximately 10% leading to surgery.
Pre-MTC, an annual average of eleven thoracic trauma operations occurred, with surgical stabilisation of rib fractures (SSRFs) constituting 47%. Post-MTC, the annual total number of operations significantly increased by an average of 33 cases (294%) [p = 0.004], and SSRFs by 30 cases (556%) [p = 0.003] and comprised 78% of total operations.
Other thoracic trauma procedures also increased, sometimes performed concomitant with SSRF, including eight debridements/evacuations of haemothorax, six diaphragm repairs, two lung repairs, and one bronchoscopy yearly. Around five cases per year required concurrent procedures by other specialties. All procedures were performed in the MTC operating theatre, without impacting the elective thoracic surgery workstream.
Conclusion: The MTC had a significant impact on thoracic trauma patient management, increasing surgical interventions but without affecting elective workload. Improved collaboration with other trauma specialties was an additional key development. 

## A351 Joint clinical oncology and thoracic surgery lung cancer clinics reduce waiting times to start treatment

## Jonathan Strickland1, Jonathan McAleese2, Rory Beattie1, Ralitsa Baranowski1, Gerard Hanna2, Donovan Campbell3

## 1Royal Victoria Hospital, Belfast, United Kingdom. 2Belfast City Hospital, Belfast, United Kingdom. 3Queen's University, Belfast, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: Jonathan Strickland
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A351
Background: For complex cases of non-small cell lung cancer, guidelines recommend assessment by both a clinical oncologist (CO) and thoracic surgeon (TS) to assess whether radical surgery or radiotherapy would be more appropriate. We piloted a joint clinic attended by both TS and CO to undertake such assessments simultaneously and aimed to assess the impact on treatment time-lines.
Methods: All patients treated with RR or RS in 2019 were assessed for evidence of referral to both TS and CO. The time from MDM referral to assessment and first oncological treatment (TTT) was determined.
Results: 15% of those undergoing RS or RR were referred to both TS and CO. 35% of these referrals were to TS first with subsequent referral to CO (sequential pathway). 65% had a simultaneous referral, 87% of these were to the joint clinic. There was a time delay between TS and CO assessments in sequential referrals; median 26 days. 41% of these cases had evidence of progression (worsening cancer or performance status), compared to 0% in the joint clinic. The TTT for those treated with RR in the joint clinic was median 47 days, and was median 64 days in the sequential group (p value 0.03). The TTT, for those treated with RS, in the joint clinic pathway was median 96 days, and in the surgery only pathway was 58 days.
Conclusions: A joint clinic demonstrates a reduction in timelines to radiotherapy treatment compared to sequential referral patterns. This reduces risk of disease progression while awaiting treatment.

## A352 The role of surgical intervention in thoracic endometriosis: a single-centre experience

## Laurence Orchard, Akshay Patel, Kumar Kunde, Janice Rymer, Shaheen Kazali, Denis Tsepov, Andrea Bille

## Guys Hospital, London, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: Laurence Orchard
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A352
Background: The thoracic cavity represents the most frequent site of extragenital endometriosis, depending on the exact site and volume of the deposits it can manifest in a wide range of presenting symptoms, such as pneumothorax, haemothorax, haemoptysis and pulmonary nodules. These are defined clinically as thoracic endometriosis syndrome (TES). TES can present in isolation, however the majority of patients presenting with TES with have concomitant pelvic disease. Despite the morbidity associated with the disease the exact mechanism behind the cause remains poorly understood. By studying the presentation, treatment, and outcomes of a case series we aim to gain further understanding into the disease process and clinical presentation.
Methods: We conducted a single-centre retrospective case-series analysis over a 24-month period. We collected all demographic, histopathological and outcome data in the short-term period.
Results: We have operated on 31 patients with TES. Presenting complaints ranged from chest pain (n = 28), catamenial pneumothorax (n = 1), haemoptysis (n = 1) and endometriotic pain (n = 1). All patients underwent diagnostic thoracic exploration via robotic (n = 29) or VATS (n = 2) approach. Twenty-five patients underwent diaphragmatic resection of deposits and reconstruction, 3 underwent additional lung resection for endometriotic deposits and 3 underwent parietal pleurectomy. Median length of hospital stay was 2 days (2–5). Long-term follow-up is being conducted to determine recurrence.
Conclusions: This represents a complex group of patients with a heterogeneous disease process and presentation. Management requires a multi-disciplinary approach in order to ensure quality of life and disease control.

## A353 Advancing the quality of thoracic surgical operative notes using a natural language processing AI-model

## Rayhaan Mohammed, Rachel Chubsey, Huzaifa Ahmad, Chantima Natawadee, Akolade Habib, Edward Caruana, Pavlos Papoulidis

## Glenfield Hospital, Leicester, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: Rachel Chubsey
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A353
Objectives: This quality improvement project assesses the feasibility of using a commercially-available Natural-Language Processing Artificial Intelligence Model (Chat GPT4.0, OpenAI, USA) for the generation of thoracic surgical operative notes. We aimed to determine compliance of AI generated notes against the Royal College of Surgeons (RCS) Good Surgical Practice guidelines.
Methods: 30 operation notes from October 2023 were chosen at random for generation using ChatGPT4.0. A pre-agreed prompt was used to generate the notes according to RCS guidelines; “Generate an operation note as per the RCS guidelines for the following procedure…”. No identifiable patient data was entered. The content of the AI-generated notes was checked against the 18 point RCS template.
Results: 30 operative notes by five thoracic surgeons at a single-centre were generated. 13 of the 18 requirements were generated with 100% accuracy in all notes, including date, time and name of procedure, incision, operative findings and complications. Blood loss was included in 75%, procedure urgency in 76%, surgeon details in 97% and postoperative antibiotic and venous thromboembolism instructions were included in 83% and 79% of notes respectively.
Conclusions: The integration of AI in the generation of operative notes is feasible and generates clear and well structured notes in line with RCS guidance. Accurate data input into the AI model is crucial. The use of a predetermined template may reduce administrative burden for healthcare professionals. Further comparative studies should aim to quantify the extent of these perceived benefits, together with any risk associated with the adoption of this technology.

## A354 Clinical outcomes of coronary endarterectomy: single centre experience

## Ammar Mustafa, Giuseppi Rescigno, John Billing, Nicolas Nikolaidis, Maciej Matuszewski, Patrick Yiu, Mahmoud Abdelaziz

## Heart and Lung Centre, Wolverhampton, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: Ammar Mustafa
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A354
Background: There is a noticeable increase in the complexity of CAD and chronicity of coronary plaques that demand Coronary Endarterectomy (CE) to achieve complete revascularisation. CE has been criticised for carrying higher risk of morbidity and mortality. In this study, we present our CE experience and clinical results.
Patients and Methods: 41 patients underwent CE in our institution between 2018 and 2023. All patients have been followed up for: mortality, in hospital and post discharge morbidity including CCS Angina class and postoperative ACS.
Results: Mean age was 65.2 ± 7.5 years and 90% were males. 51% were diabetic, 29.3% had preoperative LV dysfunction and 73% were urgent cases. 30% of patients had multiple procedures including CABG and 70% had CABG alone. 17% of patients had CE in non LAD vessel and 2 patients had CE in more than one coronary. 17% CE were achieved via the open technique using vein patch reconstruction, all of them were performed in LAD position.
Median hospital stay was 4.6 days and mean follow up was for 13 months. There was one death 4 years after the operation due to ventricular arrhythmias. 2 patients developed ACS post discharge due to progression of native vessel disease or vein graft occlusion, none of culprits was at the endarterectomised vessel.
Conclusions: Our CE short term outcome supports its use in selected patients to achieve complete revascularisation. Appropriate anticoagulation strategies might contribute to improved results. We plan to continue the follow up of our patients for the long term outcomes.

## A355 Combined aortic and endovascular clinic for patients with complex aortic disease

## Una Ahearn1, Deborah Harrington1, Mark Field1, Manoj Kuduvalli1, Ayman Kenawy1, Omar Nawaytou1, Ahmed Othman1, Francesco Torella1,2, Jonathan Smout1,2, Simon Neequaye1,2, Robert Fisher1,2, Rao Vallabhaneni1,2

## 1Liverpool Heart & Chest Hospital, Liverpool, United Kingdom. 2Liverpool University Foundation Trust, Liverpool, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: Una Ahearn
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A355
Objectives: Aortic pathologies, encompassing a spectrum of conditions from aneurysms to dissections, have long posed complex challenges in the field of Aortic surgery. Recent advancements have seen a significant shift towards minimally invasive techniques, notably endovascular and hybrid open repair, offering promising alternatives to traditional open surgical methods.
Furthermore, advancements in imaging technology, such as the use of three-dimensional imaging, have facilitated more accurate preoperative planning and improved intraoperative guidance during endovascular procedures. These technologies have enabled surgeons to better visualize the aorta and surrounding structures, leading to more successful and precise interventions.
Subsequently, this has led to an increase in the number of patients suitable for endovascular and/or hybrid intervention, in which previously conventional open surgery was prohibitive. This prompted interest in developing a comprehensive clinic model for patients.
Methods: Following CT imaging and cardiac investigations, patients are referred for case discussion to a distal aortic Multi-disciplinary Team, consisting of Aortic surgeons, Endovascular Aortic surgeons, Vascular Interventional radiologist, Cardiologist, Vasculitis physician and Aortic Advanced Practitioner.
Following MDT patients are reviewed in a multidisciplinary focused clinic by aortic and vascular surgeons, cardiologist, Intensivist and ACP. Pulmonary function tests, cognitive and frailty assessments are completed.
Results: By considering the patient's anatomy/imaging, aortic disease, overall health, lifestyle, and personal circumstances, healthcare providers can tailor effective conservative, endovascular or hybrid treatment plans.
Conclusion: This type of clinic streamlines patient care, offering various expertise under one roof for better management of complex aortic conditions and has proved to be popular with patients.

## A356 The influence of geographical location and referral timing on hospital outcome following emergency surgery for type A aortic dissection: an investigation from a national cohort

## George Gradinariu1, Hussein El-Shafei2, Renzo Pessotto3, Mark Danton1

## 1Golden Jubilee National Hospital, Glasgow, United Kingdom. 2Aberdeen Royal Infirmary, Aberdeen, United Kingdom. 3Royal Infirmary of Edinburgh, Edinburgh, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: George Gradinariu
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A356
Objective: We analysed the impact of geographical location and timing of type A aortic dissection (TAAD) diagnosis to surgery on hospital mortality using a national registry.
Methods: All patients undergoing emergency TAAD repair in 3 centres between 2008 and 2022 were included. The primary endpoint was in-hospital mortality. Times from diagnostic scan to surgery (T1) and first-imaging to surgery (T2) were recorded. We defined early (2008–2014) and recent (2015–2022) eras.
Results: 357 patients referred from 35 emergency departments across 12 health regions were included. Median diagnosis-to-surgery (T1) and overall-time (T2) were 5 h [IQR: 3.7–6.7] and 7.4 h [IQR:5–21.5] respectively. Median T1 [4.5 vs.5.8 h, p < 0.001] and T2 [6.6 vs.10.2 h, p < 0.001] were shorter in the recent era. Patients from regions without surgical centres [n = 161,47%] had longer T1 (5.5 vs.4.4 h; p < 0.001) compared to those in regions with surgical centres [n = 185,54%].
Hospital mortality was 26% [93/357], decreasing from 30% [33/109] to 24% [60/248], p = 0.24 in the recent era. Mortality was similar 24.7% [47/143] vs. 27.5% [46/121], p = 0.55, for patients referred from within vs.outside a surgical health region. Times-to-surgery and distances from home to referral/surgical centre were similar for survivors vs. non-survivors (Table). Age was associated with mortality [OR 1.05; 95%CI: 1.03–1.07, p < 0.001], while gender [OR 0.94, 95%CI: 0.57–1.57, p = 0.89] was not. 139 patients (39%) had pericardial effusion at presentation. This was associated with higher mortality [OR 1.80; 95%CI: 1.11–2.90, p = 0.02].
Conclusion: Over 14 years, time from TAAD diagnosis to surgery decreased. Patients in health regions with surgical centres had faster access to treatment. Hospital mortality was associated with patient and disease factors, and not with geography or time-to-surgery.

## A357 Evaluating local adherence to best practice for the pre-operative management of Chronic Obstructive Pulmonary Disease (COPD) patients undergoing Lung Volume Reduction Surgery (LVRS) using a standardised proforma: a clinical audit

## Aamir Amin1, Sohaib Mallick2, Hemangi Chavan3, Sofina Begum3

## Correspondence: Aamir Amin

Objectives: To assess whether local pre-operative guidelines for COPD patients undergoing LVRS are being met and to introduce the required changes.
Methods: The local pre-operative LVRS protocol stipulates full lung function tests (LFTs), CT thorax with additional quantitative analysis, ventilation-perfusion (V/Q) scan, echocardiogram and pulmonary rehabilitation within 6 months post-referral. COPD patients undergoing LVRS between October, 2022 to February, 2023 were included. Thereupon, data was collected prospectively for patients admitted between August to October, 2023.
Results: Baseline data analysis revealed that out of 13 patients, 85% (n = 11) had a CT-thorax and LFTs within 1 year pre-operatively, 15% (n = 2) had V/Q scans within 1 year, 85% (n = 11) had echocardiograms within 6 months and 69% (n = 9) had completed pulmonary rehabilitation. Post-intervention, preliminary data collection revealed that 100% (n = 4) patients had a CT thorax and LFTs within 1 year pre-operatively, 75% (n = 4) had V/Q scans within 1 year and an echocardiogram within 6 months, while 100% (n = 4) patients had completed pulmonary rehabilitation.
Conclusions: The introduction of a pre-operative LVRS proforma can significantly contribute to improving adherence to guidelines within a specified time frame. While as most patients had fulfilled the local pre-operative requirements for undergoing LVRS, there was considerable variability in the duration between investigations and procedure necessitating change. 

## A358 Remote patient monitoring for patients awaiting elective cardiac surgery: what symptoms should we monitor?

## Thanakorn Rojanathagoon1,2, Anas Boulemden3, Niki Nicou3, Adam Szafranek3, Jacob Chacko3

## 1School of Medicine, University of Nottingham, Nottingham, United Kingdom. 2Faculty of Medicine, Srinakharinwirot University, Bangkok, Thailand. 3Trent Cardiac Centre, Nottingham University Hospitals NHS Trust, Nottingham, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: Thanakorn Rojanathagoon
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A358
Patients waiting for major cardiac surgery have significant comorbidities and are at risk of morbidity and mortality whilst awaiting intervention. With prolonged waiting times, remote patient monitoring (RPM) allows for regular monitoring of patients who may demonstrate evolving symptoms and herald the risk of an adverse event. RPM and the utilization of current technology allows clinicians and healthcare systems to monitor the waiting list and amend surgical priority thereby minimizing the risk of adverse events. This study sought to guide RPM by evaluating the key symptoms and preoperative risk factors to monitor that lead to increased mortality & perioperative complications.
A literature review was conducted. Data was collected through Pubmed, Ovid MEDLINE, Embase, and Google Scholar. Articles were screened and relevant articles were extracted; results were summarized in the related section.
Eighteen manuscripts were reviewed with a total of 121,555 patients. Key outcomes of common symptoms and risk factors used to assess preoperative patients for worsening were: impaired left ventricular function (66%); unstable angina (44%); myocardial infarction, prior history of MI, or AMI (33%); atrial fibrillation, non-AF arrythmia, and ECG abnormalities (27.7%); congestive heart failure and associated symptoms (22%); and others.
Based on a review of existing literature we have identified the key risk factors associated with adverse outcomes whilst waiting cardiac surgical intervention. For RPM systems we suggest that the utilization of technology and a questionnaire specifically monitoring for the above risk factors will result in a lower rate of adverse events on the waiting list.

## A359 Should more arterial grafting be considered for coronary artery bypass grafting when the results are comparable?

## Patricia Leitch, Irfan Rasheed, Alia Noorani, Ashok Narayana, Max Baghai, Donald Whittaker, Rajit Despande, Habib Khan

## King's College Hospital, London, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: Patricia Leitch
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A359
Background: Higher survival and improved long- term patency rates are associated with use of multiple arterial grafts (MAG) during coronary artery bypass graft (CABG) surgery. Despite the literature surgeons remain cautious in use of MAG.
Methods: A retrospective review of all CABG patients at a single centre between was undertaken to identify and compare single arterial graft (SAG) and MAG patients between September 2013 to September 2023. The SAG group underwent CABG using left internal mammary (LIMA) ± veins. The MAG group had grafting using LIMA plus other artery (right internal mammary artery (RIMA) and/or radial artery) ± veins. Patients who underwent endoscopic surgery or other procedures during the same operation were excluded.
Results: 2,679 and 487 patients underwent SAG and MAG respectively. MAG patients were younger when compared to SAG (67.4 (± 11.1) vs 70.7 (± 10.4) years). Of the MAG group 72.5% (n = 353) underwent bilateral IMA (BIMA) grafting, 21.1% (n = 103) LIMA plus radial and 6.4% (n = 31) BIMA plus radial.
Results for SAG and MAG respectively: post-operative hospital stay 8.4 days (± 11.1) vs 7.9 (± 10.9), in-hospital mortality 0.6% vs 0.8%, permanent stroke 0.5% vs 0.4%, new dialysis/haemofiltration 1.2% vs 1.8%, deep sternal wound infection 0.7% vs 1.0%, reoperation for bleeding 0.6% vs 0.2% and arrythmia 22.9% vs 21.1%.
Conclusion: Our study demonstrates no difference in terms of in hospital mortality, stroke rate, sternal wound infection and post operative hospital stay between single vs multiple arterial grafts. We conclude that more arterial grafting should be considered, especially in the younger population.

## A361 Extracorporeal life support technology for rewarming victims of accidental hypothermia: lessons and insights from a Great Britain & Ireland survey

## Kate Cockayne, Soans Isabel, Rana Mehdi, Avishek Samaddar, Richard Warwick, Khurum Mazhar

## Royal Stoke, Stoke, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: Kate Cockayne
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A361
Objectives: Accidental Hypothermic Cardiac Arrest (AHCA) requiring rewarming via Extra-Corporeal Life Support (ECLS) is a rare phenomenon and presents a clinical challenge. There is currently no national standards or protocols for these patients. We reviewed our cases of AHCA and investigated the experience of individual cardiothoracic centres to address this gap.
Methods: An electronic survey was distributed to UK and Ireland clinical perfusion scientists. Results were limited to the past decade and duplicate responses excluded. Synchronously we reviewed individual notes of victims of AHCA from our Major Trauma Centre (MTC).
Results: 18 centres responded. 14 centres (78%) accepted patients for AHCA rewarming. 32 respondents’ patients have been rewarmed using ECLS (range 0–8) in the past decade. 26 patients (81%) were above 16 years of age. 10 patients (29%) were successfully weaned from ECLS (Range 0–4). 3 patients were rewarmed in the absence of cardiac arrest. However, only 4 centres (22%) said they follow a structured SOP for management of AHCA. 5/7 patients at our MTC survived to discharge (see Fig. 1). Patients that survived had a witnessed VF arrest.
Conclusions: AHCA patients who undergo ECLS rewarming are a rare occurrence but with a poor prognosis. Future studies should focus on risk stratifying this group of patients to provide an optimal triage and resuscitation evidence base to optimise patient outcomes. 

## A362 Lung volume reduction: a cost-effective treatment of chronic obstructive pulmonary disease

## Kathryn Mulryan1,2,3, Jan Sorensen4, Karen Redmond2,1

## 1Professor Eoin O'Malley National Thoracic and Transplant Centre, Mater Misericordiae University Hospital, Dublin, Ireland. 2Beacon Hospital, Beacon Court, Bracken Rd, Sandyford Business Park, Sandyford, Dublin, Ireland. 33. School of Postgraduate Studies, Royal College of Surgeons in Ireland, Dublin, Ireland. 4Health Research Outcomes Centre, Royal College of Surgeons in Ireland, Dublin, Ireland

## Correspondence: Kathryn Mulryan
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A362
Objectives: Lung volume reduction (LVR) is an effective treatment offered to patients with emphysema. LVR has recently been commissioned by the NHS England and can be carried out in a robotic assisted (RATS-LVR), video-assisted (VATS-LVR) or using endobronchial valves (EBV-LVR) approach. The cost-effectiveness of these methods has not been established.
Methods: An observational study was conducted from 2021–2022, including 31 patients in total. The primary outcome was the absolute change in health related quality of life as quantified using EQ-5D-5L and St George’s Research Questionnaire (SGRQ) for patients who underwent LVR as compared to a control group. This was used to calculate quality of life years (QALYs) which form the basis for this cost-effectiveness assessment. Costs were collected prospectively.
Results: All modalities of LVR have been found to fall under a cost-effectiveness threshold of €30,000 (Fig. 1). The mean difference in total SGRQ score was -19.61 units, indicating efficacious treatment.
Conclusions: LVR has been found to improve the quality of life for patients with COPD in this study and falls under an ‘unofficial’ threshold of €30,000/QALY. All patients meeting referral criteria should be offered screening for LVR. 

## A363 Retrospective regional study of sarcoma related lung metastases and outcomes of disease management modality in Northern Ireland

## Josh Brown, Koray Potel, Jacie Jiaqi Law, Donovan Campbell, James Cartlidge, Sandra McAllister, Alastair Graham, Rory Beattie

## Royal Victoria Hospital, Belfast, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: Josh Brown
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A363
Sarcomas represent a rare heterogeneous group of tumours with marked propensity to metastasise to the lungs. Pulmonary metastasis is an indicator of advanced disease state and disease recurrence is frequent which poses significant oncological management challenges to the multidisciplinary team. Pulmonary sarcoma can be managed with different therapeutic strategies with varied accompanying 5-year survival rates reported.
Objectives: This study examines the management of patients with metastatic pulmonary sarcoma managed through a regional multidisciplinary meeting over a 15 year period. We share the Northern Ireland experience of patient outcomes including treatment modality and mortality.
Methodology: In 2007 a sarcoma focused multidisciplinary meeting was created to centralise patients care. The database of patients that were referred to thoracic surgery was analysed using electronic records.
Results: From 2007 to 2022, 92 patients with histology confirmed metastatic pulmonary sarcoma were identified (median age: 58 years). The most prevalent subtypes were osteosarcoma, leiomyosarcoma, and Ewing’s sarcoma. 30 patients had bilateral thoracic metastases. The median survival was 14 months from identification of pulmonary metastatic disease. The mean 5-year survival rate was 38%. The greater number of identified lung metastases and re-interventions required were the most significant negative prognosticators.
Conclusions: A shift toward thoracoscopic surgery alongside the increased utilisation of adjuvant therapies has improved disease free intervals. This review demonstrates the enhanced role of the multidisciplinary approach to holistic management. Pulmonary metastases play a significant role in mortality in sarcoma patients. Early diagnosis and aggressive treatment results in increased disease-free interval and a reduced mortality.

## A364 Does robotic assisted thoracoscopic lung resection improve post-operative AKI in lung cancer patients?

## Salman Arif1, Michael Otorkpa2, Michael Gooseman1, Vasileios Tentzeris1, Suhail Qadri1, Michael Cowen1

## 1Castlehill Hospital, Cottingham, United Kingdom. 2Golden Jubilee National Hospital, Glasgow, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: Salman Arif
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A364

Objectives:
AKI is a common complication after thoracic surgery ranging from 3.1% to 16.1%.
It adversely affects the patient outcomes
The objectives of this study were to find out if robotics assisted lung resection improves post operative AKI in lung cancer patients

Methods:
All the patients undergoing lung resections for diagnosis/treatment of lung cancer from May 2022 to June 2023 were included
All the patients undergoing any procedures other than lung resection were excluded
Retrospective analysis of patient records was performed and the patients were divided into two groups i.e.; VATS and RATS
Each of the groups were subdivided into those having wedge resections or lobectomy
The primary outcome was incidence of AKI

Results:
Total Lung Resections performed during the study period were 269
VATS were 176 (65.4%) and RATS were 93 (34.6%)
Female patients were 59% in VATS and 56.99% in RATS group whereas male patients were 40.9% and 43% in VATS and RATS respectively
47.7% had wedge resection and 52.3% had lobectomies in VATS group and 39.8% had wedge and 60.2% had lobectomy among RATS group
Day1 AKI was observed in 21.7% VATS lobectomies as compared to 12.5% RATS lobectomies
Mortality was 0.6% in VATS and 0% in RATS

Conclusion:
We have found that the incidence of AKI is reduced to half in patients undergoing RATS as compared to VATS lung resections
Further studies can evaluate the impact of RATS on the quality of patient care in more detail e.g.; timing of adjuvant chemotherapy etc.

 

## A365 Incidence rates of organ-specific malperfusion and associated mortality in patients presenting with acute type A aortic dissection: a meta-analysis

## Ashwini Chandiramani1, Mohammed Al-Tawil2, Assem Elleithy3, Sahil Kakar4, Tharun Rajasekar5, Amer Harky6

## 1St Thomas' Hospital, London, United Kingdom. 2Al-Quds University, Jerusalem, Palestine. 3Royal Blackburn Hospital, Blackburn, United Kingdom. 4Queen Elizabeth Hospital Birmingham, Birmingham, United Kingdom. 5Liverpool Medical School, Liverpool, United Kingdom. 6Liverpool Heart and Chest Hospital, Liverpool, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: Ashwini Chandiramani
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A365
Objective: Acute Type A Aortic Dissection (ATAAD) is a life-threatening clinical emergency that necessitates immediate surgical intervention. When complicated by malperfusion, perioperative mortality is reported to be increased by upto 39%. The aim of this study is to investigate the epidemiology of specific types of organ malperfusion in ATAAD and to analyse the impact on survival outcomes associated with each malperfusion type.
Methods: Electronic databases PubMed, MEDLINE, and Embase were searched through to April 2023. The extracted data included patient characteristics and incidence of organ-specific malperfusion. Primary outcomes were the respective in-hospital mortality associated with each organ-specific malperfusion type. A proportional meta-analysis was conducted to pool results.
Results: A total of 34 studies met the inclusion-criteria, including a total of 28,917 patients. Peripheral limb malperfusion was most prevalent with 0.11 (95% CI [0.09, 0.14]). This was followed by iliofemoral (0.10 (95% CI [0.08,0.13]), cerebral (0.10, 95% CI [0.08, 0.12]) and coronary malperfusion (0.09, 95% CI [0.07, 0.11]). Pooled mortality with organ malperfusion varied between 13 and 33%. Within this population, the highest mortality was associated with mesenteric malperfusion (37% (95% CI [27%, 47%])). This was followed by coronary (33% (95% CI [26%, 40%])) and cerebral malperfusion (29% (95% CI [24%, 34%])). In comparison, spinal malperfusion was associated with a lower mortality rate of 13% (95% CI [4%, 25%]).
Conclusion: In-hospital survival can vary depending on the presence & type of malperfusion. Organ-specific malperfusion syndromes should be considered when assessing the peri-operative risk and surgical planning of patients undergoing surgical repair for ATAAD.sss

## A366 Addition of intra-operative variables augments the predictive power of EuroSCORE II

## Khurum Mazhar, Rana Mehdi, Avishek Samaddar, Richard Warwick, Lognathen Balacumaraswami, Marko Raseta, Qamar Abid

## Royal Stoke, Stoke, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: Khurum Mazhar
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A366
Objectives: The European System for Cardiac Operative Risk Evaluation (EuroSCORE) II is a widely used risk stratification tool to predict in-hospital mortality after cardiac surgery. However, it relies solely on pre-operative variables to determine outcomes. We sought to incorporate readily available intra-operative variables into risk modelling to assess their impact on patient outcomes.
Methods: Addition of the intra-operative variables, Duration of Operation, Cumulative Bypass time and Cumulative Aortic Cross Clamp time was used to calculate 4 short-term outcomes of interest via logistic regression analysis: In-hospital mortality, Haemofiltration/Dialysis, Stroke/Transient Ischaemic Attack and Re-sternotomy for cardiac tamponade/bleeding. Application of the ‘rule of 10’ was used to correct for sample overfitting. The models were validated using Bootstrapping and resampling techniques. Area Under Receiver Operating Curves (AUROC) were calculated for ES2 Vs ‘new’ linear and quadratic risk models.
Results: 8,008 patients analysed. Both the new quadratic and linear models outperformed ES2 to predict 30 day mortality (AUROC 0.85 Vs 0.83 Vs 0.79 respectively). Re-sternotomy for bleeding/tamponade was also more predictable with our new models c.f. ES2 (AUROC 0.67 Vs 0.65 Vs 0.63 respectively). De Long’s test for both these outcomes was significant (p < 0.05). The new models outperformed ES2 for other outcomes (Fig. 1) but De Long’s test was not significant.
Conclusion: We find that the predictive power of ES2 is enhanced with the addition of intra-operative variables. This has potential practical applications for the surgical team in decision making. 

## A367 Occult traumatic diaphragmatic defects: the importance of VATS in patients undergoing rib fixation

## Stamatina Koskolou, Jennifer Williams, Vasileios Valtzoglou, Ainis Pirtnieks, Malgorzata Kornaszewska, Tom Combellack

## University Hospital of Wales, Cardiff, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: Stamatina Koskolou
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A367
Background: Patients with blunt chest trauma and fractured ribs often have additional injuries. We noticed that, during video-assisted thoracoscopic surgery (VATS) inspection when performing rib fixation, some patients had occult injuries to other structures, such as diaphragm or lung, which had not been identified preoperatively by the CT. Traumatic diaphragmatic defects place patients at risk of developing incarcerated hernias of abdominal viscera, which convey high morbidity and mortality.
Objective: Investigate the underreporting of diaphragmatic injuries in patients who underwent rib fixation at our local Major Trauma Centre (MTC).
Method: We identified patients who underwent rib fixation in our local MTC following blunt chest trauma from 2018–2023 using local databases (PATS, Theatreman, WCP). We included patients who had concomitant rib fixation and diaphragmatic surgical intervention. Their pre-operative trauma CT reports where reviewed to compare radiological and surgical findings.
Results: We identified 14 patients in total who underwent rib fixation and concurrent diaphragm repair. The diaphragmatic injury was reported on the CT on 3 cases(21.4%), entirely unreported on 8 cases(57%) and vaguely reported on the remaining 3 cases(21.4%), whose radiological findings indicated suspicion or challenges in excluding diaphragmatic injury. The odds of detection were 0.27, while the risk of complete undetection was 0.57.
Conclusion: This study highlights the importance of VATS inspection during rib fixation to assess the diaphragm for defects that may lead to abdominal herniation. We intend to perform further analysis to explore diagnostic accuracy ofu CT and predictive factors for traumatic diaphragmatic defects in these patients.

## A368 Practising mindfulness with virtual reality: departmental study

## Natawadee Chantima1, Jade Taylor2, Oliver Pumphrey1, Akolade Habib1, Huzaifa Ahmad1, Sridhar Rathinam1

## 1Glenfield Hospital, Leicester, United Kingdom. 2University of Leicester Medical School, Leicester, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: Natawadee Chantima
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A368
Objectives: Stress and anxiety put doctors at high risk of burnouts, poor wellbeing, and reduced performance. This can be detrimental, especially during periods necessitating high level decision making.
Mindfulness is proven to have a positive impact on psychological wellbeing. The aim of this study is to assess the psychological and physiological impact of relaxation and mindfulness exercises on medical students and surgeons with virtual reality (VR).
Methods: Surgeons, doctors and students within the Thoracic Department were invited for a seven-minute mindfulness session using the Rescape VR headset. Participants completed pre- and post-experience questionnaires on wellbeing, stress and anxiety. Physiological effects before and after the experience were recorded.
Results: 11 surgeons/doctors and 5 students participated in the study. All participants found the experience enjoyable. Most agreed that the experience made them calmer (88%), less stressed (88%), and more relaxed (81%). The experience is associated with decreased stress and anxiety scores in participants whom these feelings were present. 25% of participants never allocate time to prioritise wellbeing, others allocate at least once a week. The experience encouraged 63% of participants to allocate more time for wellbeing. No significant physiological changes post-experience was observed.
Conclusions: VR experiences were associated with relaxation and reduction in stress and anxiety. This could increase technical and non-technical performance, improve wellbeing for doctors and care for patients. The headset was effective as it reduces distractions within a busy hospital environment. Selfcare and regular practice of mindfulness should be encouraged amongst all levels of doctors despite busy schedules.

## A369 Patient satisfaction in out-patient follow up appointment's following cardiac surgery: telemedicine vs face-to face consults

## Khurum Mazhar, Rana Mehdi, Isabel Soans, Richard Warwick

## Royal Stoke, Stoke, United Kingdom

## Correspondence: Khurum Mazhar
Journal of Cardiothoracic Surgery 2024, 19(2):A369
Objectives: The coronavirus 2019 (COVID-19) pandemic enabled the widespread use of Telemedicine (TM) in health care organisations. Lifting of social restrictions saw many organisations resume Face to Face (F2F) consultations. The objective of this study was to determine patient satisfaction with F2F Vs TM consults for Outpatient (OP) post-cardiac surgery patients at a single UK based, NHS University Teaching Hospital.
Methods: Patients were electronically surveyed regarding F2F/TM experiences over a 3 month period. All responses were anonymous. Satisfaction was assessed using Likert scales.
Results: Results of 73 participants (57-F2F Vs 21-TM: following exclusion criteria) were analysed. Patients were more satisfied with F2F consults when rating experience of doctor consultation (p = 0.02), however when ranking convenience and overall satisfaction with experience of the appointment no difference was found between F2F Vs TM (p = 0.83 & p = 0.90 respectively). Regression analyses of F2F consults showed journey time, cost of travel and clinic waiting time predicted satisfaction levels (p = 0.04).
Conclusion: TM was found to be non-inferior to traditional F2F consults for patient satisfaction with OP post operative cardiac surgical consults. In a post Covid era with advancing digital technologies and emphasis on sustainability for health organisations, TM potentially offers a cheaper, bio-ethical alternative to F2F approach.
